# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Verifier backend plugins (Stage 3b) — pluggable contraction soundness gates.

The llcore CPU-Verification arc concluded that the right contraction verifier is
**SDP/Lyapunov (LMI), not Z3/SMT**: for the scalar / diagonal RWKV gene the contraction
condition is closed-form (Z3 decorative), while for **coupled** (non-diagonal) genes a common
quadratic Lyapunov matrix certifies a strictly larger class than any fixed induced norm. This
module exposes that conclusion as **pluggable backends** so the evolution loop can choose its
soundness gate without hard-coding a certifier.

Backends (all *fail-closed*: an unavailable or erroring backend certifies nothing):

- :class:`ClosedFormScalarBackend` — the scalar RWKV-gene closed-form Lipschitz contraction
  (reuses :func:`llcore.verifier.verify_lipschitz_contraction`). No extra dependencies; the
  default backend.
- :class:`InfNormBackend` / :class:`TwoNormBackend` — coupled-gene induced-norm contraction
  over the achievable-t box (closed-form / vertex SVD; numpy only).
- :class:`SdpLyapunovBackend` — coupled-gene common quadratic Lyapunov LMI (cvxpy, optional
  ``[sdp]`` extra). Certifies P-weighted contractions no fixed norm captures. ``available``
  is False if cvxpy is absent, and :meth:`certifies` then returns False (fail-closed).

Gene contract (duck-typed):
- a **scalar** gene exposes ``decay``/``mix``/``gate_str`` floats (``StateUpdateGene``);
- a **coupled** gene exposes ``decay`` shape (n,) and ``W`` shape (n,n) arrays (and optional
  ``V``; defaults to identity). The coupled backends read these; they do not import any
  research module.

All coupled backends certify contraction over the *sound* achievable-t box
``t_i ∈ [t_min_i, 1]`` with ``t_min_i = sech²(Σ_j|W_ij| + max_input·Σ_j|V_ij|)`` — a tighter,
still-sound floor than the free ``[0,1]`` box. A True verdict implies ρ(J)<1 over the box.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import itertools
from typing import Any, Protocol, runtime_checkable

import numpy as np

try:
    import cvxpy as _cp

    _CVXPY_AVAILABLE = True
    # FAIL-CLOSED solver selection. cvxpy's default (SCS, first-order) returns FALSE NEGATIVES near
    # the SDP feasibility boundary — it fails to find a Lyapunov certificate that exists,
    # under-certifying the contraction region. The independent eigen re-check below guards soundness
    # (false positives) but cannot recover a missed certificate. CRITICAL: a CLARABEL-absent
    # environment must NOT silently fall back to ``_SDP_SOLVER = None`` == cvxpy default == SCS.
    # We therefore track CLARABEL presence separately: ``SdpLyapunovBackend.available`` is False
    # unless cvxpy AND CLARABEL are BOTH present, and the SDP solve path is never reached under SCS.
    _CLARABEL_AVAILABLE = "CLARABEL" in _cp.installed_solvers()
    _SDP_SOLVER = "CLARABEL" if _CLARABEL_AVAILABLE else None
except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - environment dependent
    _CVXPY_AVAILABLE = False
    _CLARABEL_AVAILABLE = False
    _SDP_SOLVER = None


def cvxpy_available() -> bool:
    """True iff cvxpy (the SDP backend's solver) is importable."""
    return _CVXPY_AVAILABLE


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Backend protocol.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #


@runtime_checkable
class VerifierBackend(Protocol):
    """A soundness gate: ``certifies(gene)`` is True iff ``gene`` is provably contracting."""

    name: str

    @property
    def available(self) -> bool: ...

    def certifies(self, gene: Any) -> bool: ...


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Coupled-gene helpers (numpy only).
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #


def _coupled_arrays(gene: Any) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
    """Extract (decay (n,), W (n,n), V (n,n)) from a duck-typed coupled gene, clipped to the
    legal box decay∈[0,1], W∈[-2,2]."""
    decay = np.clip(np.asarray(gene.decay, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1), 0.0, 1.0)
    n = decay.shape[0]
    W = np.clip(np.asarray(gene.W, dtype=np.float64).reshape(n, n), -2.0, 2.0)
    V = getattr(gene, "V", None)
    V = np.eye(n) if V is None else np.asarray(V, dtype=np.float64).reshape(n, n)
    return decay, W, V


def _t_min(decay: np.ndarray, W: np.ndarray, V: np.ndarray, max_input_abs: float) -> np.ndarray:
    M = np.abs(W).sum(axis=1) + max_input_abs * np.abs(V).sum(axis=1)
    return 1.0 - np.tanh(M) ** 2


def _jac_at_t(decay: np.ndarray, W: np.ndarray, t: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    return np.diag(decay) + np.diag((1.0 - decay) * t) @ W


def _box_vertices(t_lo: np.ndarray) -> list[np.ndarray]:
    n = t_lo.shape[0]
    return [np.array([(1.0 if b else t_lo[i]) for i, b in enumerate(c)])
            for c in itertools.product((0, 1), repeat=n)]


def _infnorm_sup(decay: np.ndarray, W: np.ndarray, t_lo: np.ndarray) -> float:
    n = decay.shape[0]
    best = 0.0
    for i in range(n):
        off = sum(abs(W[i, j]) for j in range(n) if j != i)
        for ti in (t_lo[i], 1.0):
            diag = abs(decay[i] + (1.0 - decay[i]) * ti * W[i, i])
            best = max(best, diag + (1.0 - decay[i]) * ti * off)
    return float(best)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Backends.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #


class ClosedFormScalarBackend:
    """Default backend: scalar RWKV-gene closed-form Lipschitz contraction (Stage 1b)."""

    name = "closed_form_scalar"

    @property
    def available(self) -> bool:
        from .invariants import is_z3_available  # noqa: F401 (z3 optional; closed form needs no z3)
        return True

    def certifies(self, gene: Any) -> bool:
        from .invariants import verify_lipschitz_contraction
        try:
            return verify_lipschitz_contraction(gene).contraction is True
        except Exception:
            return False


class InfNormBackend:
    """Coupled-gene induced-∞-norm contraction (closed-form, numpy only)."""

    name = "inf_norm"

    @property
    def available(self) -> bool:
        return True

    def certifies(self, gene: Any, max_input_abs: float = 1.0) -> bool:
        try:
            decay, W, V = _coupled_arrays(gene)
            return bool(_infnorm_sup(decay, W, _t_min(decay, W, V, max_input_abs)) < 1.0)
        except Exception:
            return False


class TwoNormBackend:
    """Coupled-gene induced-2-norm contraction (max σ_max over 2^n box vertices)."""

    name = "two_norm"

    @property
    def available(self) -> bool:
        return True

    def certifies(self, gene: Any, max_input_abs: float = 1.0) -> bool:
        try:
            decay, W, V = _coupled_arrays(gene)
            t_lo = _t_min(decay, W, V, max_input_abs)
            return all(float(np.linalg.svd(_jac_at_t(decay, W, v), compute_uv=False)[0]) < 1.0
                       for v in _box_vertices(t_lo))
        except Exception:
            return False


class SdpLyapunovBackend:
    """Coupled-gene common quadratic Lyapunov (LMI) contraction via cvxpy (optional [sdp]).

    Certifies a P ≻ 0 with P − J_vᵀ P J_v ≻ 0 at every t-box vertex ⇒ P-weighted contraction
    ⇒ ρ(J)<1 over the box. Strictly richer than any fixed induced norm.

    FAIL-CLOSED: ``available`` is True only when cvxpy AND CLARABEL are BOTH present. cvxpy's SCS
    default false-negatives near the feasibility boundary (the arc's pinned-away artifact); a
    CLARABEL-absent environment must NEVER silently run the SDP under SCS. The genuine SDP solve
    path is therefore guarded by ``_CLARABEL_AVAILABLE`` and refuses (returns False) when CLARABEL
    is missing. The 2-norm fast-path (P=I) is solver-independent and stays valid regardless.
    Pre-screen ρ(J_v)<1 (necessary). The solver's P is re-verified by an independent eigenvalue
    check (never solver-blind)."""

    name = "sdp_lyapunov"

    @property
    def available(self) -> bool:
        # Fail-closed: require BOTH cvxpy and CLARABEL. Without CLARABEL the SDP would otherwise run
        # under SCS (the artifact-prone solver), so this backend declares itself unavailable.
        return _CVXPY_AVAILABLE and _CLARABEL_AVAILABLE

    def certifies(self, gene: Any, max_input_abs: float = 1.0, margin: float = 1e-7) -> bool:
        try:
            decay, W, V = _coupled_arrays(gene)
            n = decay.shape[0]
            t_lo = _t_min(decay, W, V, max_input_abs)
            verts = _box_vertices(t_lo)
            Js = [_jac_at_t(decay, W, v) for v in verts]
            # 2-norm fast path (P=I) — solver-independent sound certificate; usable even without
            # cvxpy/CLARABEL for that subset.
            if all(float(np.linalg.svd(J, compute_uv=False)[0]) < 1.0 for J in Js):
                return True
            # FAIL-CLOSED: the genuine SDP solve must run under CLARABEL. Refuse if cvxpy is absent
            # OR CLARABEL is not installed — never fall through to cvxpy's SCS default.
            if not _CVXPY_AVAILABLE or not _CLARABEL_AVAILABLE:
                return False
            for J in Js:  # necessary condition for the LMI
                if float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(J)))) >= 1.0:
                    return False
            P = _cp.Variable((n, n), symmetric=True)
            I = np.eye(n)
            cons = [P >> I] + [P - J.T @ P @ J >> margin * I for J in Js]
            _cp.Problem(_cp.Minimize(_cp.trace(P)), cons).solve(solver=_SDP_SOLVER)
            if P.value is None:
                return False
            Pv = 0.5 * (P.value + P.value.T)
            if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(Pv))) <= 0.0:
                return False
            for J in Js:
                M = Pv - J.T @ Pv @ J
                if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(0.5 * (M + M.T)))) <= 0.0:
                    return False
            return True
        except Exception:
            return False


_REGISTRY = {
    "closed_form_scalar": ClosedFormScalarBackend,
    "inf_norm": InfNormBackend,
    "two_norm": TwoNormBackend,
    "sdp_lyapunov": SdpLyapunovBackend,
}


def get_verifier_backend(name: str = "closed_form_scalar") -> VerifierBackend:
    """Return a verifier backend by name. Names: ``closed_form_scalar`` (default),
    ``inf_norm``, ``two_norm``, ``sdp_lyapunov``. Raises KeyError for unknown names."""
    if name not in _REGISTRY:
        raise KeyError(f"unknown verifier backend {name!r}; available: {sorted(_REGISTRY)}")
    return _REGISTRY[name]()


def available_backends() -> dict[str, bool]:
    """Map backend name -> whether its dependencies are present (sdp_lyapunov needs cvxpy)."""
    return {name: cls().available for name, cls in _REGISTRY.items()}

# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Concrete plugins for the Verified Evolution skeleton (coupled n=2 substrate).

Provides, for :mod:`evolvable_core`:
  * :class:`CoupledGeneCodec`  — genotype <-> Track C ``CoupledGene`` (dim=6).
  * Objectives                 — RotationObjective / BenignDecayObjective /
                                 NonNormalObjective (deterministic free-response match).
  * VerifierBackends           — none / inf_norm / two_norm / sdp / union, wrapping
                                 the Track C/D certifiers, fail-closed + memoised.
  * empirical_spectral_radius  — dense (s,x)-box oracle (soundness check G1).
  * classify_region            — tightest sound certifier class for a gene.

All additive: reuses Track C/D research modules; **does NOT touch src/**.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np

# --- wire in sibling research modules (Track C / D) ------------------------- #
_HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
_RESEARCH = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(_HERE, ".."))
for _d in ("coupled_z3_contraction", "spectral_lyapunov_contraction"):
    _p = os.path.join(_RESEARCH, _d)
    if _p not in sys.path:
        sys.path.insert(0, _p)

from coupled_map import (  # noqa: E402
    CoupledGene,
    infnorm_over_box_freeT,
    jacobian,
    step,
    t_min_per_coord,
)
from two_norm_vertex_certifier import certify_2norm_contraction  # noqa: E402
from lyapunov_sdp_certifier import (  # noqa: E402
    CVXPY_AVAILABLE,
    certify_common_lyapunov,
)

# FAIL-CLOSED solver selection for the SDP. cvxpy's SCS default produces FALSE NEGATIVES near the
# feasibility boundary (it under-certifies, fabricating a too-small certified region and an inflated
# "residual"). The deeper hazard is a SILENT regression: a CLARABEL-absent environment would fall
# back to ``_SDP_SOLVER = None`` == cvxpy default == SCS, the artifact-prone solver. So we do NOT
# fall back. If CLARABEL is installed we pin it; if not, ``_CLARABEL_OK`` is False and the genuine
# SDP solve path in ``_sdp_certifies`` REFUSES (returns False) rather than running under SCS. The
# inf-/2-norm fast paths remain valid (they are solver-independent closed-form certificates).
try:
    import cvxpy as _cp  # noqa: E402
    _CLARABEL_OK = "CLARABEL" in _cp.installed_solvers()
    _SDP_SOLVER = "CLARABEL" if _CLARABEL_OK else None
except Exception:  # pragma: no cover
    _CLARABEL_OK = False
    _SDP_SOLVER = None

# Genotype layout: [decay0, decay1, W00, W01, W10, W11].
_BOX_LO = np.array([0.0, 0.0, -2.0, -2.0, -2.0, -2.0])
_BOX_HI = np.array([1.0, 1.0, 2.0, 2.0, 2.0, 2.0])
_HALF = 0.5 * (_BOX_HI - _BOX_LO)  # [0.5,0.5,2,2,2,2] per-dim mutation scale


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Gene codec.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #


class CoupledGeneCodec:
    """Genotype (6 reals) <-> coupled n=2 gene. Extension point: swap for a
    higher-dim / multi-kernel codec to evolve a different substrate."""

    dim = 6
    fallback_genotype = np.array([0.5, 0.5, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0])  # ||J||_inf=0.5<1

    def random(self, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray:
        return _BOX_LO + (_BOX_HI - _BOX_LO) * rng.random(self.dim)

    def clip(self, g: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        return np.clip(np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64), _BOX_LO, _BOX_HI)

    def to_gene(self, g: np.ndarray) -> CoupledGene:
        g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
        return CoupledGene.make(decay=g[:2], W=g[2:6].reshape(2, 2))

    def crossover(self, a: np.ndarray, b: np.ndarray, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray:
        mask = rng.random(self.dim) < 0.5
        return np.where(mask, a, b)

    def mutate(self, g: np.ndarray, sigma: float, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray:
        return g + sigma * _HALF * rng.standard_normal(self.dim)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Objectives (deterministic free-response matching). Higher fitness = better.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #


def _free_response(gene: CoupledGene, s0: np.ndarray, T: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Autonomous (x=0) trajectory s[0..T], shape (T+1, 2)."""
    s = np.asarray(s0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    x0 = np.zeros(2)
    traj = [s.copy()]
    for _ in range(T):
        s = step(gene, s, x0)
        traj.append(s.copy())
    return np.stack(traj)


def _r2(traj: np.ndarray, target: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Coefficient of determination over steps k=1..T (skip the trivial k=0 match).

    R^2 = 1 - SS_res/SS_tot in (-inf, 1]. Project-standard discriminative metric
    (Step C / E-A / ridge readout): a 'lazy decay-to-0' gene scores low/negative
    on a rotation target (does not explain its variance), so the landscape has
    headroom — no exp(-MSE) ceiling (the demo's 0.997 saturation, ③/llive trap)."""
    tr, tg = traj[1:], target[1:]
    ss_res = float(np.sum((tr - tg) ** 2))
    ss_tot = float(np.sum((tg - tg.mean(axis=0)) ** 2))
    if ss_tot < 1e-12:
        return 0.0
    return 1.0 - ss_res / ss_tot


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RotationObjective:
    """Reproduce a damped 2-D rotation from a fixed start. Rewards COUPLED
    contracting dynamics near the stability boundary (complex Jacobian
    eigenvalues => antisymmetric W => ||J||_inf > 1 even when rho<1, sigma_max<1).
    Optimum lives in the 2-norm region the inf-norm OVER-REJECTS."""

    name: str = "rotation"
    T: int = 30
    radius: float = 0.93
    period: float = 10.0
    amp: float = 0.4
    s0: tuple = (0.4, 0.0)

    def _target(self) -> np.ndarray:
        w = 2.0 * np.pi / self.period
        ks = np.arange(self.T + 1)
        a = self.amp * self.radius ** ks
        return np.stack([a * np.cos(w * ks), a * np.sin(w * ks)], axis=1)

    def fitness(self, gene: CoupledGene) -> float:
        return _r2(_free_response(gene, np.array(self.s0), self.T), self._target())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BenignDecayObjective:
    """Reproduce a simple diagonal exponential decay (no rotation). Optimum lives
    INSIDE the inf-norm region (decay~rate, W~0) => honest-null control (G5)."""

    name: str = "benign"
    T: int = 30
    rate: float = 0.85
    amp: float = 0.4
    s0: tuple = (0.4, 0.4)

    def _target(self) -> np.ndarray:
        ks = np.arange(self.T + 1)
        a = self.amp * self.rate ** ks
        return np.stack([a, a], axis=1)

    def fitness(self, gene: CoupledGene) -> float:
        return _r2(_free_response(gene, np.array(self.s0), self.T), self._target())


# Reference NON-NORMAL contracting gene (strictly lower-triangular coupling):
# rho(J)=0.5<1 (contracting) but sigma_max>1 and ||J||_inf>1 (both fixed norms
# reject) => SDP-only region. The target is its free response; matching it needs
# a gene in the SDP-only region, so only the SDP gate can reach the optimum.
_NONNORMAL_REF = CoupledGene.make(decay=[0.5, 0.5], W=[[0.0, 0.0], [1.6, 0.0]])


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class NonNormalObjective:
    """Reproduce the free response of a non-normal contracting reference (transient
    amplification then decay). Designed so the optimum is SDP-CERTIFIABLE but
    inf-norm AND 2-norm REJECTED — a capability test for whether the SDP gate can
    reach dynamics the conservative gates forbid. (Stated honestly: this task is
    built to live in the SDP-only region; it is a reachability test, not a claim
    that natural tasks are non-normal.)"""

    name: str = "nonnormal"
    T: int = 30
    s0: tuple = (0.5, 0.5)

    def _target(self) -> np.ndarray:
        return _free_response(_NONNORMAL_REF, np.array(self.s0), self.T)

    def fitness(self, gene: CoupledGene) -> float:
        return _r2(_free_response(gene, np.array(self.s0), self.T), self._target())


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Verifier backends (fail-closed contraction gates). Memoised on rounded genotype.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #


def _key(gene: CoupledGene, nd: int = 6) -> tuple:
    g = gene.clipped()
    return (tuple(np.round(g.decay, nd)), tuple(np.round(g.W.reshape(-1), nd)))


def _inf_certifies(gene: CoupledGene) -> bool:
    """Closed-form ||J||_inf < 1 over the achievable-t box (== Z3 inf-norm)."""
    g = gene.clipped()
    t_lo = t_min_per_coord(g, max_input_abs=1.0)
    return bool(infnorm_over_box_freeT(g, t_lo=t_lo) < 1.0)


def _two_certifies(gene: CoupledGene) -> bool:
    return bool(certify_2norm_contraction(gene, t_domain="tmin1").certified)


def _vertex_jacobians(gene: CoupledGene):
    """The 4 t-box-vertex Jacobians J(t) = diag(decay)+diag((1-decay)t)W, t in
    {t_min,1}^2. The achievable-t set is inside this box (sound over-approx)."""
    g = gene.clipped()
    t_lo = t_min_per_coord(g, max_input_abs=1.0)
    verts = ((t_lo[0], t_lo[1]), (t_lo[0], 1.0), (1.0, t_lo[1]), (1.0, 1.0))
    return [np.diag(g.decay) + np.diag((1.0 - g.decay) * np.asarray(t)) @ g.W for t in verts]


def _sdp_certifies(gene: CoupledGene) -> bool:
    """SDP common-Lyapunov, BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING fast path.

    Short-circuits (all sound, decisions identical to a bare solve):
      1. inf-norm or 2-norm certifies => contraction (subset of SDP, no solve).
      2. rho(J_v) >= 1 at ANY t-box vertex => the vertex LMI P - J_v^T P J_v >> 0
         is INFEASIBLE (discrete-Lyapunov necessity), so the common-P SDP cannot
         exist => reject WITHOUT a solve. This prunes the (many) non-contracting
         children that would otherwise each pay a full cvxpy canonicalisation.
      3. otherwise run the genuine SDP.
    Fail-closed if cvxpy unavailable/None."""
    if _inf_certifies(gene) or _two_certifies(gene):
        return True
    if not CVXPY_AVAILABLE:
        return False
    # FAIL-CLOSED: the genuine SDP solve must run under CLARABEL. If CLARABEL is not installed we
    # REFUSE rather than silently solving under SCS (solver=None == cvxpy default == SCS), which
    # false-negatives near the feasibility boundary. The inf-/2-norm fast paths above already
    # handled every solver-independent certificate, so this only declines the genuine-SDP residual.
    if not _CLARABEL_OK:
        return False
    # necessary condition for common quadratic stability: rho<1 at every vertex.
    for J in _vertex_jacobians(gene):
        if float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(J)))) >= 1.0:
            return False
    r = certify_common_lyapunov(gene, t_domain="tmin1", solver=_SDP_SOLVER)
    return r.certified is True


class _Backend:
    def __init__(self, name: str, fn):
        self.name = name
        self._fn = fn
        self._cache: dict[tuple, bool] = {}

2:"""Richer VerifierBackend — degree-4 (non-quadratic) Lyapunov via the symmetric 2nd power.
3:
4:Targets the **Track-D D4 residual**: genes with ρ(J)<1 over the box that NO common *quadratic*
5:Lyapunov P (and no fixed induced norm) certifies. A degree-4 homogeneous Lyapunov
6:V(z)=m(z)^T P m(z), where m(z) is the vector of degree-2 monomials (the Veronese map), certifies
7:a STRICTLY LARGER class (Parrilo-Jadbabaie SOS hierarchy, k=2). We use the *lifted* common-P
8:LMI on the symmetric 2nd Kronecker power J^[2] of each box-vertex Jacobian:
9:
10:    find P ≻ 0  s.t.  P - (J_v^[2])^T P (J_v^[2]) ≻ 0   at every t-box vertex v.
11:
12:Requiring this as a FULL matrix inequality on the lifted space R^{n(n+1)/2} is SOUND (sufficient
13:for contraction of the original map, since V(z) then strictly decreases) though slightly
14:conservative vs the exact SOS-on-the-variety condition — i.e. a valid degree-4 Lyapunov
15:certificate. When feasible where the plain quadratic SDP is not, it certifies a D4-residual gene.
16:
17:This is a NEW VerifierBackend plug-in for the skeleton — only the backend changes; evolve() and
18:the codecs are untouched. cvxpy required (degrades to None if absent).
19:"""
20:from __future__ import annotations
21:
22:import itertools
23:
24:import numpy as np
25:
26:try:
27:    import cvxpy as cp
28:    # FAIL-CLOSED solver selection. cvxpy's SCS default gives FALSE NEGATIVES near the feasibility
29:    # boundary (the cause of the spurious deg4/deg6 complementarity), but the deeper hazard is a
30:    # silent regression: a CLARABEL-ABSENT environment would fall back to ``solver=None`` == cvxpy
31:    # default == SCS, the exact artifact-prone solver. So we do NOT fall back. If CLARABEL is
32:    # installed we pin it; if it is NOT, ``_SOLVER`` is left None as a sentinel and ``_CLARABEL_OK``
33:    # is False, and every certifier below REFUSES to certify (returns False) rather than running
34:    # under SCS. INVARIANT: when CLARABEL is present (this env) behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged.
35:    _CLARABEL_OK = "CLARABEL" in cp.installed_solvers()
36:    _CVXPY = True
37:    _SOLVER = cp.CLARABEL if _CLARABEL_OK else None
38:except Exception:  # pragma: no cover
39:    _CVXPY = False
40:    _CLARABEL_OK = False
41:    _SOLVER = None

43:
44:def _mono_basis(n: int) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
45:    """Degree-2 monomial index pairs (i<=j), the Veronese basis of dim n(n+1)/2."""
46:    return [(i, j) for i in range(n) for j in range(i, n)]
47:
48:
49:def sym2_power(A: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
50:    """Symmetric 2nd Kronecker power A^[2]: the matrix s.t. under z->Az the degree-2 monomial
51:    vector m(z) (basis i<=j, value z_i z_j) maps as m(Az) = A^[2] m(z). dim = n(n+1)/2.
52:
53:    Row r = monomial (i,j): m_r(Az) = (Az)_i (Az)_j = Σ_{k,l} A_ik A_jl z_k z_l. Collect onto the
54:    symmetric basis (k,l)->(min,max): for an unordered pair {p,q} with p<q the coefficient is
55:    A_ip A_jq + A_iq A_jp; for p==p it is A_ip A_jp.
56:    """
57:    A = np.asarray(A, dtype=np.float64)
58:    n = A.shape[0]
59:    basis = _mono_basis(n)
60:    idx = {pair: c for c, pair in enumerate(basis)}
61:    m = len(basis)
62:    S = np.zeros((m, m))
63:    for r, (i, j) in enumerate(basis):
64:        for k in range(n):
65:            for l in range(n):
66:                p, q = (k, l) if k <= l else (l, k)
67:                S[r, idx[(p, q)]] += A[i, k] * A[j, l]
68:    return S
69:
70:
71:def certify_deg4(vertex_jacobians: list[np.ndarray], margin: float = 1e-7) -> bool:
72:    """Common degree-4 (symmetric-2nd-power) Lyapunov over a set of vertex Jacobians.
73:
74:    Sound: if feasible, V(z)=m(z)^T P m(z) is a valid Lyapunov ⇒ the map contracts over the box.
75:    Independent eigenvalue re-check of P (never solver-blind). Necessary pre-screen: ρ(J_v)<1.
76:
77:    FAIL-CLOSED: if cvxpy is absent OR CLARABEL is not installed, REFUSE to certify (return False)
78:    rather than silently solving under SCS (which false-negatives near the feasibility boundary and
79:    is the artifact-prone solver the arc pinned away from).
80:    """
81:    if not _CVXPY or not _CLARABEL_OK:
82:        return False
83:    for J in vertex_jacobians:
84:        if float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(J)))) >= 1.0:
85:            return False
86:    lifts = [sym2_power(J) for J in vertex_jacobians]
87:    m = lifts[0].shape[0]
88:    P = cp.Variable((m, m), symmetric=True)
89:    I = np.eye(m)
90:    cons = [P >> I] + [P - L.T @ P @ L >> margin * I for L in lifts]
91:    try:
92:        cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cp.trace(P)), cons).solve(solver=_SOLVER)
93:    except Exception:
94:        return False
95:    if P.value is None:
96:        return False
97:    Pv = 0.5 * (P.value + P.value.T)
98:    if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(Pv))) <= 0.0:
99:        return False
100:    for L in lifts:
101:        M = Pv - L.T @ Pv @ L
102:        if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(0.5 * (M + M.T)))) <= 0.0:
103:            return False
104:    return True

116:        p = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(here, "..", d))
117:        if p not in sys.path:
118:            sys.path.insert(0, p)

121:    t_lo = t_min_per_coord(g, max_input_abs=1.0)
122:    verts = [np.array([t_lo[0], t_lo[1]]), np.array([t_lo[0], 1.0]),
123:             np.array([1.0, t_lo[1]]), np.array([1.0, 1.0])]
124:    return [np.diag(g.decay) + np.diag((1.0 - g.decay) * v) @ g.W for v in verts]

1:# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
2:"""Pilot A — Rump verified positive-definiteness + OR-of-{CLARABEL,SCS} standing gate.
3:
4:Purely ADDITIVE research code (research/verified_evolution_sdp_gate/). Does NOT touch src/.
5:Zero new dependencies (numpy only). Does NOT change the behaviour of any existing committed
6:certifier — it is a new, stricter, SELF-CONTAINED gate layered *beside* them.
7:
8:Motivation (see ``VERIFICATION_METHODS_SURVEY_2026-06-03.md`` Pilot A,
9:``AUDIT_SCS_CLARABEL_2026-06-03.md``, memory ``feedback_cvxpy_pin_accurate_solver``):
10:
11:  * The arc's SDP certifiers re-check the solver's claimed Lyapunov matrix ``P`` (and each
12:    decrease LMI ``P - J^T P J``) with a FLOATING-POINT ``np.linalg.eigvalsh`` test
13:    ``min_eig > 0``. Near the feasibility boundary that 1e-7-scale test is float-fragile.
14:  * cvxpy's default SCS solver returns FALSE NEGATIVES near the boundary; CLARABEL recovers
15:    them. The audit pinned CLARABEL, but a *single* solver is still a single point of failure.
16:
17:This module hardens BOTH:
18:
19:  1. :func:`verified_pd` — a Rump-style VERIFIED positive-definiteness test. A successful
20:     floating-point Cholesky of ``M - alpha*I`` together with a RIGOROUS Cholesky backward-error
21:     bound PROVES ``lambda_min(M) >= (alpha - error_bound) > 0``. SOUND by construction: it
22:     returns ``ok=True`` only when ``M`` is provably PD. Under-certifying a barely-PD ``M`` is
23:     acceptable; a FALSE POSITIVE (certifying a non-PD ``M``) is forbidden.
24:
25:  2. :func:`or_certifies_lyapunov` — solve the common-Lyapunov SDP under EACH solver
26:     (CLARABEL and SCS), and accept iff ANY solver returns a ``P`` whose ``P`` AND every
27:     ``P - J_v^T P J_v - margin*I`` pass the Rump verified-PD test. This is an OR (not a vote):
28:     a vote would preserve the SCS false negative; an OR bakes "solver-swap = decisive detector"
29:     into a standing gate.
30:
31:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32:THE RIGOROUS CHOLESKY ERROR BOUND (soundness derivation)
33:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
34:We use the classical floating-point Cholesky backward-error result (Wilkinson; Demmel,
35:*Applied Numerical Linear Algebra* Thm 10.7; Higham, *Accuracy and Stability of Numerical
36:Algorithms* Thm 10.3 / 10.4; Rump 2006, "Verification of positive definiteness", BIT).
37:
38:If the floating-point Cholesky factorization of a symmetric ``n x n`` matrix ``A`` (unit roundoff
39:``u``) RUNS TO COMPLETION (no non-positive pivot, no nan/inf), then there exists a perturbation
40:``E`` with computed factor ``R`` such that ``R^T R = A + E`` EXACTLY and
41:
42:        |E_ij| <= gamma_{n+1} * sqrt(A_ii) * sqrt(A_jj),      gamma_k := k*u / (1 - k*u),
43:
44:(provided ``(n+1)*u < 1``). A standard, simple-to-state consequence (and the form the survey
45:quotes) bounds the spectral norm of ``E`` by
46:
47:        ||E||_2 <= gamma_{n+1} * max_i A_ii * n        (loose, via |E_ij| <= gamma * maxdiag)
48:
49:We use the slightly tighter, still-rigorous Frobenius/2-norm bound
50:        ||E||_2 <= ||E||_F <= gamma_{n+1} * sum_i A_ii  ==  gamma_{n+1} * trace(A)
51:because ``|E_ij| <= gamma_{n+1} sqrt(A_ii A_jj)`` gives
52:        ||E||_F^2 = sum_ij E_ij^2 <= gamma_{n+1}^2 (sum_i sqrt(A_ii))^2 ... -> we use the
53:plain, unambiguously-correct envelope ``||E||_2 <= gamma_{n+1} * n * max_i A_ii`` so the proof
54:needs no Cauchy-Schwarz subtlety. (For our 2x2 / 3x3 matrices the difference is immaterial; we
55:optimise SOUNDNESS and clarity over tightness, exactly as the spec permits.)
56:
57:Since ``R^T R = A + E`` is a Cholesky product it is positive SEMI-definite, so
58:        lambda_min(A + E) >= 0  =>  lambda_min(A) >= -||E||_2 >= -bound(A).
59:
60:Apply this to ``A = M - alpha*I``: a successful Cholesky proves
61:        lambda_min(M - alpha*I) >= -bound(M - alpha*I)
62:        => lambda_min(M) >= alpha - bound(M - alpha*I).
63:If ``alpha - bound(M - alpha*I) > 0`` then ``M`` is PROVABLY positive definite and that quantity
64:is a guaranteed lower bound on ``lambda_min(M)``.
65:
66:The diagonal of ``A = M - alpha*I`` is ``M_ii - alpha``. ``bound`` depends on ``max_i A_ii``; to
67:keep the bound a sound OVER-estimate regardless of sign we use ``max(max_i A_ii, 0)`` clamped at
68:the original ``max_i M_ii`` (since ``alpha > 0`` shrinks the diagonal, ``max_i(M_ii - alpha) <=
69:max_i M_ii``; using ``max_i M_ii`` is therefore a sound upper envelope and is what we feed the
70:bound — never an under-estimate). This makes the guarantee hold for every alpha we try.
71:"""
72:from __future__ import annotations
73:
74:import os
75:import sys
76:
77:import numpy as np
78:
79:# IEEE-754 double unit roundoff u = 2^-53.
80:_U = float(np.finfo(np.float64).eps) / 2.0  # eps = 2^-52, u = 2^-53
81:
82:
83:# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
84:# 1) Rump-style verified positive-definiteness.
85:# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
86:
87:
88:def _gamma(k: int) -> float:
89:    """gamma_k = k*u / (1 - k*u), the standard floating-point error accumulation factor.
90:
91:    Returns +inf if k*u >= 1 (bound vacuous; caller treats as 'cannot certify')."""
92:    ku = k * _U
93:    if ku >= 1.0:
94:        return float("inf")
95:    return ku / (1.0 - ku)
96:
97:
98:def _cholesky_error_bound(maxdiag: float, n: int) -> float:
99:    """Rigorous upper bound on ||E||_2 for the computed Cholesky factor R with R^T R = A + E,
100:    where ``maxdiag`` is a sound upper bound on max_i A_ii and A is n x n.
101:
102:    Uses the unambiguous envelope ||E||_2 <= gamma_{n+1} * n * max_i A_ii (see module docstring).
103:    ``maxdiag`` is clamped to be non-negative (a non-positive maxdiag means the Cholesky pivot is
104:    already non-positive, so Cholesky would fail anyway; the bound is then 0 and irrelevant)."""
105:    md = max(float(maxdiag), 0.0)
106:    g = _gamma(n + 1)
107:    if not np.isfinite(g):
108:        return float("inf")
109:    return g * n * md
110:
111:
112:def _spd_cholesky(A: np.ndarray) -> bool:
113:    """True iff floating-point Cholesky of A runs to completion (A numerically SPD).
114:
115:    numpy raises LinAlgError on a non-positive pivot; we also reject any non-finite entry."""
116:    if not np.all(np.isfinite(A)):
117:        return False
118:    try:
119:        np.linalg.cholesky(A)
120:        return True
121:    except np.linalg.LinAlgError:
122:        return False
123:
124:
125:def verified_pd(M: np.ndarray, *, n_alpha: int = 60) -> tuple[bool, float]:
126:    """Rump-style VERIFIED positive-definiteness of symmetric ``M``.
127:
128:    Returns ``(ok, lam_min_lb)``:
129:      * ``ok``        True  => ``M`` is PROVABLY positive definite (sound; no false positives).
130:                      False => could not prove PD (M may be PD-but-too-close, indefinite, or
131:                               non-finite). Under-certification only; never a false positive.
132:      * ``lam_min_lb`` a GUARANTEED lower bound on ``lambda_min(M)`` when ``ok`` is True
133:                       (> 0); when ``ok`` is False it is the best (possibly <= 0) lower bound
134:                       found, for diagnostics only.
135:
136:    Method (see module docstring for the soundness proof):
137:      symmetrize M; for a decreasing schedule of shifts ``alpha`` attempt a floating-point
138:      Cholesky of ``M - alpha*I``. A successful Cholesky + the rigorous error bound proves
139:      ``lambda_min(M) >= alpha - bound > 0``. We try the LARGEST alpha first and return the
140:      first (hence largest) guaranteed positive lower bound.
141:    """
142:    M = np.asarray(M, dtype=np.float64)
143:    if M.ndim != 2 or M.shape[0] != M.shape[1]:
144:        raise ValueError("verified_pd expects a square matrix")
145:    n = M.shape[0]
146:    # Symmetrize (the caller may pass a numerically-asymmetric matrix; the verified statement is
147:    # about the symmetric part, which is what PD-ness of a quadratic form depends on).
148:    M = 0.5 * (M + M.T)
149:    if not np.all(np.isfinite(M)):
150:        return False, float("-inf")
151:
152:    maxdiag_M = float(np.max(np.diag(M))) if n > 0 else 0.0
153:    # bound depends only on a sound upper envelope of max diag; since every alpha we try is >= 0,
154:    # max_i(M_ii - alpha) <= max_i M_ii, so feeding max_i M_ii is a SOUND over-estimate for all alpha.
155:    err_bound = _cholesky_error_bound(maxdiag_M, n)
156:    if not np.isfinite(err_bound):
157:        return False, float("-inf")
158:
159:    # Scale reference for the alpha schedule: the spectral scale of M. ||M||_2 <= sqrt(n)*||M||_F-ish;
160:    # use the Frobenius norm as a cheap, safe upper proxy for choosing where to probe alpha.
161:    scale = float(np.linalg.norm(M)) + err_bound
162:    if scale <= 0.0:
163:        scale = 1.0
164:
165:    I = np.eye(n)
166:
167:    # Try alpha from large (~scale) down toward the error bound. The largest alpha whose shifted
168:    # Cholesky succeeds gives the largest guaranteed lower bound alpha - err_bound. We MUST require
169:    # alpha > err_bound for a positive guarantee.
170:    #
171:    # Geometric schedule between ~scale and just above err_bound, plus a fine tail near err_bound.
172:    alphas = []
173:    hi = scale
174:    lo = max(err_bound * (1.0 + 1e-12), err_bound + np.finfo(np.float64).tiny)
175:    if hi <= lo:
176:        hi = lo * 4.0
177:    # geometric descent
178:    for k in range(n_alpha):
179:        frac = k / max(n_alpha - 1, 1)
180:        alphas.append(hi * (lo / hi) ** frac)
181:    # ensure monotone-decreasing & unique
182:    alphas = sorted(set(alphas), reverse=True)
183:
184:    best_lb = float("-inf")
185:    for alpha in alphas:
186:        if alpha <= err_bound:
187:            break  # cannot yield a positive guarantee
188:        A = M - alpha * I
189:        if _spd_cholesky(A):
190:            lb = alpha - err_bound
191:            if lb > 0.0:
192:                return True, float(lb)
193:            best_lb = max(best_lb, lb)
194:
195:    # No positive-guaranteeing alpha succeeded. As a last, still-sound attempt: a bare Cholesky of
196:    # M itself proves lambda_min(M) >= -err_bound (only useful as a diagnostic LB, NOT a PD proof).
197:    if _spd_cholesky(M):
198:        best_lb = max(best_lb, -err_bound)
199:    return False, float(best_lb)

203:# 2) OR-of-{CLARABEL,SCS} Rump-verified Lyapunov gate.
204:# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
205:
206:# Wire in the sibling SDP certifier (Track D) for the cvxpy problem + vertex Jacobians.
207:_HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
208:_RESEARCH = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(_HERE, ".."))
209:for _d in ("coupled_z3_contraction", "spectral_lyapunov_contraction"):
210:    _p = os.path.join(_RESEARCH, _d)
211:    if _p not in sys.path:
212:        sys.path.insert(0, _p)
213:if _HERE not in sys.path:
214:    sys.path.insert(0, _HERE)

216:
217:def _vertex_jacobians(gene, t_domain: str = "tmin1", max_input_abs: float = 1.0):
218:    """The t-box vertex Jacobians J(t) for a Track-C/D CoupledGene (n=2).
219:
220:    Reuses the SAME vertex construction as ``coupled_components`` / ``lyapunov_sdp_certifier`` so
221:    the Rump recheck verifies exactly the LMIs the SDP solver was asked to satisfy."""
222:    from coupled_map import t_min_per_coord
223:    g = gene.clipped()
224:    if t_domain == "free01":
225:        t_lo = np.zeros(2)
226:    elif t_domain == "tmin1":
227:        t_lo = t_min_per_coord(g, max_input_abs=max_input_abs)
228:    else:  # pragma: no cover
229:        raise ValueError(f"unknown t_domain {t_domain!r}")
230:    verts = [np.array([t_lo[0], t_lo[1]]), np.array([t_lo[0], 1.0]),
231:             np.array([1.0, t_lo[1]]), np.array([1.0, 1.0])]
232:    return [np.diag(g.decay) + np.diag((1.0 - g.decay) * v) @ g.W for v in verts]
233:
234:
235:def rump_verify_certificate(P: np.ndarray, vertex_jacobians, *, margin: float = 1e-7) -> bool:
236:    """VERIFIED check of a claimed common-Lyapunov certificate ``P`` over a vertex set.
237:
238:    Accept iff ``P`` AND every ``(P - J^T P J - margin*I)`` are Rump-verified positive definite.
239:    This is the SOUND replacement for the float ``eigvalsh(...) > 0`` recheck: a True here is a
240:    machine-checked guarantee, not a solver claim. SOUND: no false positives (verified_pd has none).
241:    """
242:    P = np.asarray(P, dtype=np.float64)
243:    P = 0.5 * (P + P.T)
244:    ok_P, _ = verified_pd(P)
245:    if not ok_P:
246:        return False
247:    for J in vertex_jacobians:
248:        J = np.asarray(J, dtype=np.float64)
249:        M = P - J.T @ P @ J - margin * np.eye(P.shape[0])
250:        M = 0.5 * (M + M.T)
251:        ok_M, _ = verified_pd(M)
252:        if not ok_M:
253:            return False
254:    return True
255:

257:def or_certifies_lyapunov(gene, t_domain: str = "tmin1",
258:                          solvers: tuple = ("CLARABEL", "SCS"),
259:                          *, margin: float = 1e-7,
260:                          max_input_abs: float = 1.0,
261:                          return_detail: bool = False):
262:    """OR-of-solvers Rump-verified common-Lyapunov gate.
263:
264:    For EACH solver in ``solvers``: solve the common-Lyapunov SDP (reusing
265:    ``lyapunov_sdp_certifier.certify_common_lyapunov``), take the returned ``P``, and accept the
266:    solver iff ``P`` AND every ``(P - J_v^T P J_v - margin*I)`` pass :func:`verified_pd` (Rump).
267:
268:    Returns True if ANY solver yields a Rump-passing certificate (OR -- NOT a vote; a vote would
269:    preserve the SCS false negative). Fail-closed if cvxpy is unavailable.
270:
271:    If ``return_detail`` is True, returns ``(ok, detail)`` where ``detail`` is a per-solver dict
272:    of {'solved': bool, 'status': str, 'rump_pd': bool}.
273:    """
274:    from lyapunov_sdp_certifier import CVXPY_AVAILABLE, certify_common_lyapunov
275:
276:    detail: dict = {}
277:    if not CVXPY_AVAILABLE:
278:        return (False, detail) if return_detail else False
279:
280:    import cvxpy as cp
281:    installed = set(cp.installed_solvers())
282:
283:    verts = _vertex_jacobians(gene, t_domain=t_domain, max_input_abs=max_input_abs)
284:
285:    any_ok = False
286:    for sv in solvers:
287:        d = {"solved": False, "status": None, "rump_pd": False}
288:        if sv not in installed:
289:            d["status"] = "not_installed"
290:            detail[sv] = d
291:            continue
292:        # Solve under this specific solver. certify_common_lyapunov already does its own float
293:        # eigvalsh recheck, but we IGNORE its certified flag and re-verify P with Rump ourselves so
294:        # the gate's verdict rests on the machine-checked test, not the float one.
295:        r = certify_common_lyapunov(gene, t_domain=t_domain, margin=margin,
296:                                    max_input_abs=max_input_abs, solver=sv)
297:        d["status"] = r.solver_status
298:        if r.P is not None:
299:            d["solved"] = True
300:            P = np.asarray(r.P, dtype=np.float64)
301:            d["rump_pd"] = bool(rump_verify_certificate(P, verts, margin=margin))
302:            if d["rump_pd"]:
303:                any_ok = True
304:        detail[sv] = d
305:
306:    return (any_ok, detail) if return_detail else any_ok
307:

314:    ok, lb = verified_pd(spd)

2:"""PART 2 — ADDITIVE Rump+OR HARDENED verifier factory (2026-06-03 hardening).
3:
4:A NEW, additive verifier factory that hardens the SDP certifier's solver-trust recheck WITHOUT
5:changing any default certifier's behaviour. It layers two validated, adversarially-proven-sound
6:primitives from :mod:`rump_pd` on top of the existing common-Lyapunov SDP:
7:
8:  1. :func:`rump_pd.verified_pd` — a machine-GUARANTEED positive-definiteness test (Rump-style
9:     verified Cholesky backward-error bound; 11 tests, a 36860-trial skeptic battery, 0 false
10:     positives). Replaces the solver-trusting float ``np.linalg.eigvalsh(...) > 0`` recheck with a
11:     test whose True verdict is a proof, not a solver claim.
12:
13:  2. :func:`rump_pd.or_certifies_lyapunov` — OR-of-{CLARABEL,SCS}: accept iff ANY solver returns a
14:     ``P`` that passes the Rump recheck. (An OR, not a vote — a vote would preserve a solver's false
15:     negative; an OR turns "solver-swap" into a decisive detector.)
16:
17:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
18:CRITICAL INVARIANT — match the float path's ACTUAL recheck (NO margin)
19:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
20:The audit found a trap: the SDP CONSTRAINT carries a strict margin (``P - J^T P J >> margin*I``),
21:but the certifier's internal FLOAT recheck in
22:``lyapunov_sdp_certifier.certify_common_lyapunov`` tests the **no-margin** matrices::
23:
24:    eig_P  = min eig(P)              > 0        # P strictly PD, no margin
25:    min_dec = min eig(P - J^T P J)   > 0        # decrease LMI strictly PD, NO margin subtracted
26:
27:So to PRESERVE-OR-GROW the admit set (never shrink it), the Rump recheck here MUST verify the
28:SAME matrices — ``P`` and every ``P - J^T P J`` — at the SAME (zero) margin. We therefore call
29:``rump_pd.rump_verify_certificate(P, verts, margin=0.0)`` (margin=0 ⇒ it verifies exactly
30:``P`` and ``P - J^T P J``, identical to the float test's matrices). Subtracting ``margin*I`` here
31:would test a STRICTLY HARDER condition than the float path and could spuriously shrink the admit
32:set — the exact failure mode the spec warns against. Because ``verified_pd`` is a SOUND lower bound
33:on ``lambda_min`` (``verified_pd >= float-eigvalsh`` on identical matrices, already proven), the
34:Rump recheck on the IDENTICAL matrices is a SUPERSET-or-equal of the float recheck: the admit set
35:is preserved-or-grown, NEVER shrunk, and stays SOUND (no false positives).
36:
37:This module is PURELY ADDITIVE: it does NOT touch src/, does NOT modify ``rump_pd.py``, and does
38:NOT change the behaviour of any existing committed certifier (``_sdp_certifies``, ``certify_deg*``,
39:``SdpLyapunovBackend``, ...). It is a new factory beside them; promotion to the default is a
40:SEPARATE human decision (see RUMP_HARDENING_VERDICT.md), not flipped here.

61:def rump_recheck_matches_float_path(P, vertex_jacobians) -> bool:
62:    """Rump verified-PD recheck of a claimed common-Lyapunov ``P`` over a vertex set, testing the
63:    SAME matrices the certifier's internal FLOAT recheck tests: ``P`` strictly PD AND every
64:    ``P - J^T P J`` strictly PD, at the SAME (zero) margin the float path uses.
65:
66:    Delegates to the validated :func:`rump_pd.rump_verify_certificate` with ``margin=0.0`` so the
67:    matrices verified are byte-for-byte the float path's matrices (it subtracts ``margin*I``;
68:    margin=0 ⇒ no subtraction). A True here is a machine-checked guarantee that the IDENTICAL
69:    condition the float recheck judged is satisfied — never a solver claim.
70:
71:    Because ``verified_pd`` is a sound under-estimator of ``lambda_min`` that dominates the float
72:    ``eigvalsh`` test on identical matrices, this recheck is a SUPERSET-or-equal of the float
73:    recheck: admit set preserved-or-grown, never shrunk; sound (no false positives).
74:    """
75:    from rump_pd import rump_verify_certificate
76:    return bool(rump_verify_certificate(np.asarray(P, dtype=np.float64),
77:                                        vertex_jacobians, margin=_FLOAT_PATH_MARGIN))

80:def make_sdp_verifier_rump_or(t_domain: str = "tmin1", *, margin: float = 1e-7,

87:        identical to the existing certifiers — needs no solver), OR

98:    interior, matching the existing pipeline), but the ACCEPTANCE recheck is the no-margin Rump test

125:                                        max_input_abs=max_input_abs, solver=sv)

2:"""Richer VerifierBackend — degree-6 (non-quadratic) Lyapunov via the symmetric 3rd power.
3:
4:The next rung above ``verifier_deg4`` on the verifier-fitness frontier the arc identified:
5:
6:    inf-norm → 2-norm → quadratic SDP → degree-4 Lyapunov → **degree-6 Lyapunov** → …(JSR-exact)…
7:
8:A degree-6 homogeneous Lyapunov V(z)=m3(z)ᵀ P m3(z), where m3(z) is the vector of degree-3
9:monomials (the Veronese map of order 3), certifies contraction via a common-P LMI on the
10:**symmetric 3rd Kronecker power** J^[3] of each t-box vertex Jacobian (Parrilo–Jadbabaie SOS
11:hierarchy, k=3):
12:
13:    find P ≻ 0  s.t.  P - (J_v^[3])ᵀ P (J_v^[3]) ≻ 0   at every t-box vertex v.
14:
15:SOUNDNESS — exactly as ``verifier_deg4``: the lifted FULL-space LMI on R^{C(n+2,3)} is a
16:*sufficient* (slightly conservative) form of the exact degree-6 SOS-on-the-variety condition,
17:hence a valid degree-6 Lyapunov certificate of contraction of the original map. The certifier
18:NEVER trusts the solver blindly: P's eigenvalues and the decrease-LMI eigenvalues are
19:re-checked independently, and ρ(J_v)<1 is a necessary pre-screen at every vertex.
20:
21:SOLVER NOTE (retraction — see DEG6_VERDICT.md): an earlier draft reported the degree-4/6 lifted
22:certificates were *non-nested* (complementary). That was a **cvxpy SCS solver artifact** — SCS
23:(the default first-order solver) returns FALSE NEGATIVES near the feasibility boundary, fabricating
24:a fake complementarity. Under the accurate interior-point solver CLARABEL (now pinned via
25:``_SOLVER``) the lifted ladder is **NESTED** (deg4 ⊆ deg6) and the common quadratic SDP already
26:certifies ~95% of contracting genes; degree-4/6 add only a tiny residual. ``make_deg6_verifier_n2``
27:returns the union (sdp ∪ deg4 ∪ deg6) for completeness, but with an accurate solver deg6 dominates.
28:
29:This is a NEW VerifierBackend plug-in for the skeleton — only the backend changes; ``evolve()``
30:and the codecs are untouched. cvxpy required (degrades to None/False if absent: fail-closed).
31:"""
32:from __future__ import annotations
33:
34:import itertools
35:
36:import numpy as np
37:
38:try:
39:    import cvxpy as cp
40:    # FAIL-CLOSED solver selection. cvxpy's default for these feasibility-boundary SDPs is SCS
41:    # (first-order ADMM), which produces FALSE NEGATIVES near the feasibility boundary — it fails to
42:    # find a certificate that exists. That fabricated an apparent deg4/deg6 "complementarity" (an
43:    # SCS artifact; under CLARABEL the lifted ladder is NESTED). The independent eigen re-check
44:    # guards soundness but cannot recover a missed certificate, so the solver default — not the
45:    # re-check — is the fix. CRITICAL: a CLARABEL-absent environment must NOT silently fall back to
46:    # ``solver=None`` == cvxpy default == SCS. We therefore do NOT fall back: if CLARABEL is missing,
47:    # ``_CLARABEL_OK`` is False and every certify_* below REFUSES to certify (returns False).
48:    _CLARABEL_OK = "CLARABEL" in cp.installed_solvers()
49:    _CVXPY = True
50:    _SOLVER = cp.CLARABEL if _CLARABEL_OK else None
51:except Exception:  # pragma: no cover
52:    _CVXPY = False
53:    _CLARABEL_OK = False

56:# reuse the n=2 vertex construction + the degree-4 certifier (additive, DRY).
57:from verifier_deg4 import _vertices_n2, cert_deg4_n2

59:
60:def mono_basis(n: int, degree: int) -> list[tuple[int, ...]]:
61:    """Sorted degree-`degree` monomial multi-indices over n vars; dim = C(n+degree-1, degree)."""
62:    return list(itertools.combinations_with_replacement(range(n), degree))
63:
64:
65:def sym_power(A: np.ndarray, degree: int) -> np.ndarray:
66:    """Symmetric `degree`-th Kronecker power A^[degree]: the matrix s.t. under z->Az the
67:    degree-`degree` monomial vector m(z) (basis = sorted multisets, value Π z_{β_i}) maps as
68:    m(Az) = A^[degree] m(z). dim = C(n+degree-1, degree).
69:
70:    Row r = monomial β=(β_1..β_d): m_r(Az) = Π_i (Az)_{β_i} = Σ_{p∈[n]^d} (Π_i A_{β_i,p_i}) z_{p}
71:    collected onto the symmetric basis sorted(p). Generalises ``verifier_deg4.sym2_power``
72:    (degree=2) and is brute-force-verified for n=2,3,4 and degree=2,3 (max error ~1e-16).
73:    """
74:    A = np.asarray(A, dtype=np.float64)
75:    n = A.shape[0]
76:    basis = mono_basis(n, degree)
77:    idx = {b: c for c, b in enumerate(basis)}
78:    m = len(basis)
79:    S = np.zeros((m, m))
80:    for r, beta in enumerate(basis):
81:        for p in itertools.product(range(n), repeat=degree):
82:            coeff = 1.0
83:            for i in range(degree):
84:                coeff *= A[beta[i], p[i]]
85:            S[r, idx[tuple(sorted(p))]] += coeff
86:    return S
87:
88:
89:def certify_degN(vertex_jacobians: list[np.ndarray], degree: int, margin: float = 1e-7) -> bool:
90:    """Common degree-(2*degree) homogeneous Lyapunov over a set of vertex Jacobians, via the
91:    symmetric `degree`-th power lift. degree=2 ≡ degree-4 (cf. verifier_deg4), degree=3 ≡ degree-6.
92:
93:    Sound: if feasible, V(z)=m(z)ᵀ P m(z) is a valid Lyapunov ⇒ the map contracts over the box.
94:    Independent eigenvalue re-check of P AND of every decrease-LMI (never solver-blind).
95:    Necessary pre-screen: ρ(J_v)<1 at every vertex.
96:
97:    FAIL-CLOSED: if cvxpy is absent OR CLARABEL is not installed, REFUSE to certify (return False)
98:    rather than silently solving under SCS (the artifact-prone first-order solver).
99:    """
100:    if not _CVXPY or not _CLARABEL_OK:
101:        return False
102:    for J in vertex_jacobians:
103:        if float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(J)))) >= 1.0:
104:            return False
105:    lifts = [sym_power(J, degree) for J in vertex_jacobians]
106:    m = lifts[0].shape[0]
107:    P = cp.Variable((m, m), symmetric=True)
108:    I = np.eye(m)
109:    cons = [P >> I] + [P - L.T @ P @ L >> margin * I for L in lifts]
110:    try:
111:        cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cp.trace(P)), cons).solve(solver=_SOLVER)
112:    except Exception:
113:        return False
114:    if P.value is None:
115:        return False
116:    Pv = 0.5 * (P.value + P.value.T)
117:    if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(Pv))) <= 0.0:
118:        return False
119:    for L in lifts:
120:        M = Pv - L.T @ Pv @ L
121:        if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(0.5 * (M + M.T)))) <= 0.0:
122:            return False
123:    return True
124:
125:
126:def certify_deg6(vertex_jacobians: list[np.ndarray], margin: float = 1e-7) -> bool:
127:    """Common degree-6 (symmetric-3rd-power) Lyapunov over a set of vertex Jacobians."""
128:    return certify_degN(vertex_jacobians, degree=3, margin=margin)

167:        basis = mono_basis(n, 3)
168:        m = np.array([np.prod([z[i] for i in b]) for b in basis])
169:        Az = A @ z
170:        m_true = np.array([np.prod([Az[i] for i in b]) for b in basis])
171:        err = float(np.max(np.abs(sym_power(A, 3) @ m - m_true)))
172:        print(f"sym_power(.,3) n={n} max error vs brute force: {err:.2e} (should be ~0)")

2:"""Track D — common quadratic Lyapunov (SDP/LMI) contraction certificate via cvxpy.
3:
4:Purely ADDITIVE research code (research/spectral_lyapunov_contraction/). Does NOT touch src/.
5:
6:This is the TIGHTEST quadratic certificate for the affine-in-t Jacobian family. We seek a single
7:common Lyapunov matrix P >> 0 such that, at every t-box VERTEX J_v,
8:      J_v^T P J_v - P << 0       (discrete-time quadratic stability LMI).
9:Because J(t) is AFFINE in t over the box, the vertex set is the set of extreme matrices; quadratic
10:stability at all vertices implies stability for every J in the convex hull (the whole achievable-t
11:box), by convexity of the quadratic form V(z)=z^T P z and the standard polytopic-LMI argument.
12:
13:If such P exists, the map is contractive in the P-weighted norm ||z||_P = sqrt(z^T P z): there is a
14:rate gamma < 1 with ||J_v z||_P <= gamma ||z||_P for all vertices, hence for all achievable J, hence
15:rho(J) < 1 over the box. This is a strictly RICHER class than any single induced norm (||.||_2 is the
16:special case P = I; the SDP can find a non-identity P that the 2-norm vertex test cannot).
17:
18:SOLVER territory: this is a genuine SDP/LMI and needs cvxpy. If cvxpy is not importable, this module
19:degrades gracefully: CVXPY_AVAILABLE = False and certify_common_lyapunov(...) returns
20:available=False (NEVER raises, NEVER fabricates a verdict).

42:    _CVXPY_IMPORT_ERROR = None
43:    # FAIL-CLOSED default solver. cvxpy's bare default is SCS (first-order), which FALSE-NEGATIVES
44:    # near the SDP feasibility boundary (the 2026-06-03 audit artifact). We pin the accurate
45:    # CLARABEL as the default. CRITICALLY, if CLARABEL is NOT installed we do NOT fall back to
46:    # ``_DEFAULT_SOLVER = None`` (== cvxpy default == SCS): that would silently regress to the
47:    # artifact-prone solver. Instead ``_CLARABEL_OK`` is False, and when a caller does NOT pass an
48:    # explicit ``solver=`` the function REFUSES (returns available=False / certified=None) rather
49:    # than running under SCS. A caller may STILL pass ``solver="SCS"`` EXPLICITLY (the PART-2
50:    # OR-of-{CLARABEL,SCS} gate does this, then re-verifies the result with the Rump verified-PD
51:    # test) — an explicit choice is the caller's responsibility and is honoured. See memory
52:    # feedback_cvxpy_pin_accurate_solver.
53:    _CLARABEL_OK = "CLARABEL" in cp.installed_solvers()
54:    _DEFAULT_SOLVER = "CLARABEL" if _CLARABEL_OK else None
55:except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover - environment dependent
56:    CVXPY_AVAILABLE = False
57:    _CVXPY_VERSION = None
58:    _CVXPY_IMPORT_ERROR = repr(exc)
59:    _CLARABEL_OK = False
60:    _DEFAULT_SOLVER = None
61:
62:
63:def _box_vertices(t_lo: np.ndarray) -> list[np.ndarray]:
64:    t_lo = np.asarray(t_lo, dtype=np.float64).reshape(2)
65:    return [
66:        np.array([t_lo[0], t_lo[1]]),
67:        np.array([t_lo[0], 1.0]),
68:        np.array([1.0, t_lo[1]]),
69:        np.array([1.0, 1.0]),
70:    ]
71:
72:
73:def _jac_at_t(gene: CoupledGene, t: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
74:    g = gene.clipped()
75:    t = np.asarray(t, dtype=np.float64).reshape(2)
76:    return np.diag(g.decay) + np.diag((1.0 - g.decay) * t) @ g.W

79:@dataclass(frozen=True)
80:class LyapResult:
81:    available: bool                 # was cvxpy importable AND did the solve run
82:    certified: bool | None          # True/False if available; None if not available / solver error
83:    domain: str
84:    solver_status: str | None       # cvxpy problem status (e.g. "optimal", "infeasible") or note
85:    P: list | None                  # the found P (2x2) as nested list, if certified
86:    min_eig_margin: float | None    # min eigenvalue of (P - J_v^T P J_v) over vertices (should be > 0)

88:
89:def certify_common_lyapunov(gene: CoupledGene, t_domain: str = "tmin1",
90:                            max_input_abs: float = 1.0,
91:                            margin: float = 1e-7,
92:                            solver: str | None = None) -> LyapResult:
93:    """Solve the common-P vertex-LMI feasibility SDP. Degrades gracefully if cvxpy is absent.
94:
95:    Feasibility encoded as an SDP: find P with P >> margin*I and
96:        P - J_v^T P J_v >> margin*I   at every vertex v.
97:    (Strict LMIs approximated by a fixed positive margin; reported in min_eig_margin.)
98:    """
99:    if not CVXPY_AVAILABLE:
100:        return LyapResult(available=False, certified=None, domain=t_domain,
101:                          solver_status="cvxpy_unavailable", P=None, min_eig_margin=None)
102:
103:    # FAIL-CLOSED: if the caller did not pick a solver AND CLARABEL is not installed, REFUSE rather
104:    # than letting the solve fall through to cvxpy's SCS default (the artifact-prone first-order
105:    # solver). An EXPLICIT ``solver=`` from the caller is always honoured (e.g. the OR-of-solvers
106:    # gate that deliberately probes SCS and then Rump-re-verifies the result).
107:    if solver is None and not _CLARABEL_OK:
108:        return LyapResult(available=False, certified=None, domain=t_domain,
109:                          solver_status="clarabel_unavailable_fail_closed",
110:                          P=None, min_eig_margin=None)
111:
112:    import cvxpy as cp
113:
114:    g = gene.clipped()
115:    if t_domain == "free01":
116:        t_lo = np.zeros(2)
117:    elif t_domain == "tmin1":
118:        t_lo = t_min_per_coord(g, max_input_abs=max_input_abs)
119:    else:  # pragma: no cover
120:        raise ValueError(f"unknown t_domain {t_domain!r}")
121:
122:    verts = _box_vertices(t_lo)
123:    Js = [_jac_at_t(g, v) for v in verts]
124:
125:    P = cp.Variable((2, 2), symmetric=True)
126:    I2 = np.eye(2)
127:    # The LMI system is HOMOGENEOUS in P (scale-invariant), so a bare feasibility problem is
128:    # ill-posed: the conic solver treats the strict `>> margin*I` as `>=` and can return the
129:    # trivial P=0 (which our eigenvalue re-check would then reject) or report spurious infeasible
130:    # when paired with an upper cap. Fix the scale with `P >> I` (forces PD AND normalizes), and
131:    # minimize trace(P) to pull P toward the smallest valid Lyapunov matrix. This is well-posed.
132:    constraints = [P >> I2]
133:    for J in Js:
134:        # P - J^T P J >> margin I  (discrete Lyapunov decrease at this vertex)
135:        constraints.append(P - J.T @ P @ J >> margin * I2)
136:    prob = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cp.trace(P)), constraints)
137:
138:    try:
139:        _eff_solver = solver if solver is not None else _DEFAULT_SOLVER  # fail-safe to CLARABEL
140:        prob.solve(solver=_eff_solver) if _eff_solver else prob.solve()
141:    except Exception as exc:  # solver hiccup -> report, do not crash the sweep
142:        return LyapResult(available=True, certified=None, domain=t_domain,
143:                          solver_status=f"solver_error:{type(exc).__name__}", P=None,
144:                          min_eig_margin=None)
145:
146:    status = prob.status
147:    if status in ("optimal", "optimal_inaccurate") and P.value is not None:
148:        Pv = np.asarray(P.value, dtype=np.float64)
149:        Pv = 0.5 * (Pv + Pv.T)  # symmetrize numerically
150:        # Verify the certificate independently of the solver's claim.
151:        eig_P = float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(Pv)))
152:        min_dec = np.inf
153:        for J in Js:
154:            M = Pv - J.T @ Pv @ J
155:            M = 0.5 * (M + M.T)
156:            min_dec = min(min_dec, float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(M))))
157:        # Certified only if P is genuinely PD and every decrease LMI is genuinely PD.
158:        ok = (eig_P > 0.0) and (min_dec > 0.0)
159:        return LyapResult(available=True, certified=bool(ok), domain=t_domain,
160:                          solver_status=status, P=Pv.tolist(),
161:                          min_eig_margin=float(min(eig_P, min_dec)))
162:    else:
163:        # infeasible / unbounded / solver_error etc. -> no common quadratic P found
164:        return LyapResult(available=True, certified=False, domain=t_domain,
165:                          solver_status=status, P=None, min_eig_margin=None)
166:

1:# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
2:"""STRONG soundness test of the lifted degree-4/6 certifier — via product expansion (JSR), not
3:just pointwise ρ.
4:
5:Why this is the right test (skeptic's foundational concern): the lifted LMI imposes Lyapunov
6:decrease only at the t-box VERTICES {J_v}. The nonlinear map's mean Jacobian J̄(s)=∫₀¹J(τs)dτ lies
7:in conv{J_v}. For a QUADRATIC Lyapunov (k=1), vertex feasibility ⇒ hull feasibility by matrix
8:convexity (Aᵀ P A convex in A). For degree≥2 the lift A^[k] is non-convex in A, so vertex
9:feasibility does NOT directly imply hull decrease of THIS V. Soundness instead rests on:
10:  common vertex Lyapunov ⇒ JSR{J_v} < 1  ⇒ (JSR convex-hull theorem) JSR{conv J_v} < 1
11:  ⇒ the nonlinear map (Jacobians in the hull) contracts.
12:So a NECESSARY consequence of a SOUND certificate is: every certified gene has JSR{J_v} < 1, hence
13:JSR_lower_bound (max over products of ρ(∏)^{1/k}) < 1. If ANY deg4/deg6-certified gene has
14:JSR_lb ≥ 1, a product of vertex Jacobians expands ⇒ the certifier is UNSOUND (a real bug).
15:
16:This is strictly stronger than the pointwise-ρ@50k oracle (which can miss switched expansion).

42:from verifier_deg4 import _vertices_n2  # noqa: E402

44:
45:def jsr_lb(vertices, max_len=6) -> float:
46:    V = [np.asarray(M, dtype=np.float64) for M in vertices]
47:    best = 0.0
48:    for k in range(1, max_len + 1):
49:        for combo in itertools.product(range(len(V)), repeat=k):
50:            P = np.eye(V[0].shape[0])
51:            for i in combo:
52:                P = V[i] @ P
53:            best = max(best, float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(P)))) ** (1.0 / k))
54:    return best

65:    cert_jsr = []

1:# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
2:"""JSR bracket — the theoretical limit of the SOS Lyapunov coverage ladder (CPU).
3:
4:The verifier ladder certifies a common lifted Lyapunov over the t-box vertex set {J_v}; this
5:certifies the joint spectral radius JSR{J_v} < 1 (the exact contraction rate of the box-switched
6:linearisation, which bounds the nonlinear map by the convex-hull theorem). The degree-d SOS
7:Lyapunov is a CPU approximation of JSR; this module brackets the true JSR per gene:
8:
9:  * LOWER bound (Gripenberg)      jsr_lb = max over length-≤K vertex products of ρ(∏)^{1/k}.
10:  * UPPER bound (SOS-degree-d)    gamma_star_d = smallest γ such that {J_v/γ} admits a common
11:    degree-d lifted Lyapunov (bisection; `sym_power(J/γ,k)=sym_power(J,k)/γ^k` ⇒ monotone in γ).
12:
13:True JSR ∈ [jsr_lb, gamma_star_d]. A gene is **certified contracting at the SOS-degree-d limit**
14:iff gamma_star_d < 1. As d grows the upper bound decreases toward JSR (Parrilo–Jadbabaie), so the
15:ladder closes the residual up to its near-boundary tail. "Exact JSR" is NP-hard — we report a
16:tight *bracket*, never an exact value, and never call a gene expansive unless jsr_lb ≥ 1.
17:"""
18:from __future__ import annotations
19:
20:import itertools
21:
22:import numpy as np
23:
24:from verifier_deg6 import certify_degN  # general degree-d lifted Lyapunov (degree=2→deg4, 3→deg6, 4→deg8)
25:
26:
27:def jsr_lower_bound(vertices, max_len: int = 6) -> float:
28:    V = [np.asarray(M, dtype=np.float64) for M in vertices]
29:    best = 0.0
30:    for k in range(1, max_len + 1):
31:        for combo in itertools.product(range(len(V)), repeat=k):
32:            P = np.eye(V[0].shape[0])
33:            for i in combo:
34:                P = V[i] @ P
35:            best = max(best, float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(P)))) ** (1.0 / k))
36:    return best
37:
38:
39:def _certifies_scaled(vertices, degree: int, gamma: float, margin: float = 1e-7) -> bool:
40:    """Common degree-(2*degree) Lyapunov for the γ-scaled vertices {J_v/γ}."""
41:    scaled = [np.asarray(J, dtype=np.float64) / gamma for J in vertices]
42:    return certify_degN(scaled, degree, margin=margin)
43:
44:
45:def jsr_upper_bound_sos(vertices, degree: int, lo: float | None = None,
46:                        hi: float = 3.0, tol: float = 1e-3, margin: float = 1e-7) -> float:
47:    """gamma_star_d = smallest γ∈[lo,hi] s.t. {J_v/γ} admits a common degree-(2*degree) Lyapunov.
48:
49:    Monotone: larger γ ⇒ smaller scaled matrices ⇒ easier to certify. Bisection. lo defaults to the
50:    spectral-radius floor max_v ρ(J_v) (JSR ≥ that). Returns float('inf') if not certifiable at hi.
51:    """
52:    V = [np.asarray(J, dtype=np.float64) for J in vertices]
53:    rho_floor = max(float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(J)))) for J in V)
54:    lo = rho_floor if lo is None else max(lo, rho_floor)
55:    # guard: must be certifiable at hi, else the bound is > hi.
56:    if not _certifies_scaled(V, degree, hi, margin):
57:        return float("inf")
58:    lo_g, hi_g = lo, hi
59:    # ensure lo is infeasible (push down a touch below rho_floor where pre-screen fails)
60:    if _certifies_scaled(V, degree, lo_g, margin):
61:        return lo_g  # already certifiable at the floor (γ* ≈ rho_floor)
62:    while hi_g - lo_g > tol:
63:        mid = 0.5 * (lo_g + hi_g)
64:        if _certifies_scaled(V, degree, mid, margin):
65:            hi_g = mid
66:        else:
67:            lo_g = mid
68:    return hi_g
69:
70:
71:def jsr_bracket(vertices, degree: int = 4, max_len: int = 6) -> dict:
72:    """Bracket the JSR of {J_v}: [jsr_lb, gamma_star_d]. degree=4 ⇒ degree-8 SOS upper bound."""
73:    lb = jsr_lower_bound(vertices, max_len=max_len)
74:    ub = jsr_upper_bound_sos(vertices, degree, lo=lb)
75:    return {"jsr_lb": lb, "gamma_star": ub, "degree_lift": degree,
76:            "certified_contracting": ub < 1.0, "bracket_width": (ub - lb) if np.isfinite(ub) else None}
77:

87:def jsr_certifies_n2(gene, degree: int = 4) -> bool:
88:    """Certify contraction of an n=2 gene at the SOS-degree-(2*degree) JSR limit (γ*_d < 1)."""
89:    return jsr_upper_bound_sos(_vertices_n2(gene), degree, lo=None) < 1.0

69:            continue

46:
47:def certify(vertices, degree: int, solver, margin: float = 1e-7) -> bool:
48:    for J in vertices:
49:        if float(np.max(np.abs(np.linalg.eigvals(J)))) >= 1.0:
50:            return False
51:    lifts = [sym_power(J, degree) for J in vertices]
52:    m = lifts[0].shape[0]
53:    P = cp.Variable((m, m), symmetric=True)
54:    I = np.eye(m)
55:    cons = [P >> I] + [P - L.T @ P @ L >> margin * I for L in lifts]
56:    try:
57:        cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cp.trace(P)), cons).solve(solver=solver)
58:    except Exception:
59:        return False
60:    if P.value is None:
61:        return False
62:    Pv = 0.5 * (P.value + P.value.T)
63:    if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(Pv))) <= 0.0:
64:        return False
65:    for L in lifts:
66:        M = Pv - L.T @ Pv @ L
67:        if float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(0.5 * (M + M.T)))) <= 0.0:
68:            return False
69:    return True

codex
I found a more serious theoretical gap in the Rump narrative: the code and docs repeatedly claim `verified_pd` is a superset of the float `eigvalsh>0` check, but the implementation is a conservative proof procedure. That kind of verifier can match or undershoot float acceptance; it cannot be justified as a formal superset from the derivation alone.

34:def _true_min_eig(M: np.ndarray) -> float:
35:    M = 0.5 * (np.asarray(M, dtype=np.float64) + np.asarray(M, dtype=np.float64).T)
36:    return float(np.min(np.linalg.eigvalsh(M)))

39:def _random_symmetric(rng: np.random.Generator, n: int, shift: float) -> np.ndarray:
40:    """Random symmetric n x n matrix with a controllable diagonal shift (shift>0 pushes PD)."""
41:    A = rng.standard_normal((n, n))
42:    S = 0.5 * (A + A.T)
43:    return S + shift * np.eye(n)

160:    """Over a broad battery spanning PD / PSD / indefinite, assert Rump NEVER certifies a matrix
161:    whose exact ``eigvalsh`` min eigenvalue is <= 0 (a false positive). This is the cardinal
162:    soundness invariant: under-certification is fine, a false positive is forbidden."""
163:    rng = np.random.default_rng(2024)
164:    n_total = 0

171:        # Span the boundary: shifts from clearly-indefinite to clearly-PD, plus deliberately
172:        # near-singular cases (shift chosen to put min eig within +-1e-7 of 0).
173:        mode = rng.integers(0, 3)
174:        S = 0.5 * (lambda A: A + A.T)(rng.standard_normal((n, n)))
175:        if mode == 0:
176:            M = S + float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 4.0)) * np.eye(n)
177:        elif mode == 1:

180:            target = float(rng.uniform(-1e-6, 1e-6))

182:        else:
183:            # scaled indefinite (mixed sign eigenvalues likely).
184:            M = S * float(rng.uniform(0.1, 3.0))
185:        n_total += 1

187:        rump_ok, lb = verified_pd(M)
188:        if true_min > 0:
189:            n_pd += 1
190:        else:
191:            n_indef += 1
192:        if rump_ok:
193:            n_rump_true += 1
194:            # FALSE POSITIVE check: Rump said PD but exact min eig <= 0.
195:            if true_min <= 0.0:
196:                false_positives.append({"true_min": true_min, "lb": lb, "n": n})
197:            # When Rump certifies, its returned lower bound must be SOUND (<= true min eig).
198:            assert lb <= true_min + 1e-12, (
199:                f"unsound lower bound: lb={lb} > true_min={true_min}")
200:    assert n_total >= 200, "battery must exercise 200+ matrices"
201:    assert false_positives == [], f"Rump FALSE POSITIVES (forbidden): {false_positives}"
202:    # Sanity: the battery actually spanned both PD and indefinite, and Rump certified a healthy
203:    # fraction of the genuinely-PD ones (otherwise the soundness test is vacuous).
204:    assert n_pd > 30 and n_indef > 30
205:    assert n_rump_true > 0

codex
1. `high` — challenges claim 1 (`COMPLETENESS`).
`research/spectral_lyapunov_contraction/lyapunov_sdp_certifier.py:107-140` still contains a bare `prob.solve()` branch. It is guarded against `solver is None` when CLARABEL is absent, but a falsy non-`None` caller value such as `solver=""` bypasses the fail-closed check at `:107-110`, sets `_eff_solver` falsy at `:139`, and falls into `prob.solve()` at `:140` anyway. On top of that, the reviewed arc explicitly runs `solver="SCS"` in the OR gate (`research/verified_evolution_sdp_gate/rump_hardened_verifier.py:117-126`, `research/verified_evolution_sdp_gate/rump_pd.py:286-301`). So “every cvxpy `.solve()` is pinned to an accurate solver” is false as written. This overturns headline 1 as stated, though not the broader SDP thesis.

2. `high` — challenges claim 2 and claim 6 (`SOUNDNESS BOTH DIRECTIONS`, `JSR ORACLE`).
The CLARABEL path is not solver-blind, but it is also not rigorously sound in the way the prose claims. `lyapunov_sdp_certifier.py:147-159` accepts both `optimal` and `optimal_inaccurate`, then gates admission with plain float `eigvalsh > 0` on `P` and `P-JᵀPJ`; that is a numerical recheck, not a proof. The JSR script is only a one-sided bug detector: it says a certified gene with `jsr_lb >= 1` would expose unsoundness (`verify_certifier_jsr_soundness.py:12-14,64-72`), but `jsr_lb` is only a finite-length lower bound over products up to length 6 (`:45-54`), so `0 certified genes with jsr_lb>=1` does not prove soundness. The audit overstates this at `research/AUDIT_SCS_CLARABEL_2026-06-03.md:7,35`. This overturns the “soundness confirmed” wording, but only refines the central thesis: you have evidence of no observed false admits, not a proof that CLARABEL introduced none.

3. `high` — challenges claim 4 (`RUMP+OR preserve-or-grow`).
The “preserved-or-grown, never shrunk” rationale is overstated. `rump_hardened_verifier.py:27-35,71-73` repeatedly claims `verified_pd` “dominates” the float eigentest, but the implementation in `rump_pd.py:125-199` is a conservative proof procedure: it can under-certify barely-PD matrices. The tests do not prove a theorem; they only show no losses on comfortably-PD random matrices (`test_rump_pd.py:121-151`, note the explicit `COMFORT = 1e-6`) and on a 40-gene battery (`test_rump_hardened_verifier.py:61-85`). Worse, the raw OR helper in `rump_pd.py` verifies `P-JᵀPJ-margin*I` (`rump_pd.py:235-254,257-301`), which is strictly harder than the float path; only the wrapper in `rump_hardened_verifier.py:57-77` switches to the no-margin matrices. So the observed `286 == 286` result is empirical (`rump_hardening_compare_results.json:11-15`), not a consequence of the derivation. This overturns the hardening rationale, not the measured equal-count result.

4. `medium` — challenges claim 3 (`DEGREE-LADDER RETRACTION`) only at the documentation level.
The retraction itself looks supported: the immutable SCS-era snapshot reproduces the artifact (`verify_solver_artifact.py:11-19,83-119`), and the live CLARABEL ladder shows `deg4_minus_deg6 = 0`, `deg6_minus_deg4 = 1`, residual `10`, and `+4` over quadratic (`exp_deg6_ladder_results.json`). But `verifier_deg6.py:141-143` still claims the degree-4/6 certificates are “complementary (non-nested),” which directly contradicts both the live result and the verdict (`DEG6_VERDICT.md:59`). This does not overturn the retraction; it shows the implementation comments still encode the pre-retraction story.

5. `low` — challenges claim 4 (`RUMP+OR non-vacuous exercise`) only weakly.
The compare script itself does not instrument how many admits came through the SDP+Rump branch (`exp_rump_hardening_compare.py:92-142`). The non-vacuity is only inferable indirectly from the same-seed EXP-A ladder, where `149` of the `286` admits are `sdp_only` (`exp_deg6_ladder_results.json`). So the branch was almost certainly exercised, but the verdict text should say that explicitly rather than implying the compare artifact alone proves it. This only refines a secondary claim.

Verdict: no finding overturns the central thesis that SDP/Lyapunov is the right verifier and is about 95% complete on this CPU pool, but the publication over-claims fail-closed completeness and soundness proof strength; those claims need to be narrowed before release.
tokens used
91,360
1. `high` — challenges claim 1 (`COMPLETENESS`).
`research/spectral_lyapunov_contraction/lyapunov_sdp_certifier.py:107-140` still contains a bare `prob.solve()` branch. It is guarded against `solver is None` when CLARABEL is absent, but a falsy non-`None` caller value such as `solver=""` bypasses the fail-closed check at `:107-110`, sets `_eff_solver` falsy at `:139`, and falls into `prob.solve()` at `:140` anyway. On top of that, the reviewed arc explicitly runs `solver="SCS"` in the OR gate (`research/verified_evolution_sdp_gate/rump_hardened_verifier.py:117-126`, `research/verified_evolution_sdp_gate/rump_pd.py:286-301`). So “every cvxpy `.solve()` is pinned to an accurate solver” is false as written. This overturns headline 1 as stated, though not the broader SDP thesis.

2. `high` — challenges claim 2 and claim 6 (`SOUNDNESS BOTH DIRECTIONS`, `JSR ORACLE`).
The CLARABEL path is not solver-blind, but it is also not rigorously sound in the way the prose claims. `lyapunov_sdp_certifier.py:147-159` accepts both `optimal` and `optimal_inaccurate`, then gates admission with plain float `eigvalsh > 0` on `P` and `P-JᵀPJ`; that is a numerical recheck, not a proof. The JSR script is only a one-sided bug detector: it says a certified gene with `jsr_lb >= 1` would expose unsoundness (`verify_certifier_jsr_soundness.py:12-14,64-72`), but `jsr_lb` is only a finite-length lower bound over products up to length 6 (`:45-54`), so `0 certified genes with jsr_lb>=1` does not prove soundness. The audit overstates this at `research/AUDIT_SCS_CLARABEL_2026-06-03.md:7,35`. This overturns the “soundness confirmed” wording, but only refines the central thesis: you have evidence of no observed false admits, not a proof that CLARABEL introduced none.
